# BeThere - Master Chronological Project Context

*Auto-assembled corpus for writing the DRP Project Documentation: the **HCD Techniques Portfolio (80%)**, the **Project Pitch Leaflet (10%)**, and the **Copyright/Legal Issues Report (10%)**.*

This file interleaves every dated, substantive event from the team's working record - WhatsApp, Discord, git commits, Linear issues, Trello - into one timeline, plus research/milestone anchors. Whole-document artifacts (interview transcripts, the survey, slide decks, course rubrics, repo docs) are **not** inlined; they are indexed in the Appendix for the doc-generation step to open directly.

## Team roster (alias map) - the 4 team members
- **Gong** - Nelson Gong / Leixin Gong (WhatsApp) | gong8 (Discord) | gonglx8 (Linear) | gong-queue
- **Lukas** - luCas traCimas (WhatsApp, stylized) | lxkast (Discord) | lukastrakim (Linear) | lukas-queue
- **Noah** - Noah Seymour (WhatsApp) | seventhspark_40504 (Discord) | noah-queue
- **James** - James Hughff (WhatsApp) | the_jlh (Discord) | james.lee.hughff (Linear) | james-queue
- *"egg (i love claude)"* = WhatsApp group/system actor, not a person (filtered out)

> **Do not confuse team members with research participants.** Names like Luca Morgan, Tom Carvell, Zack Foreman, Felicity Turner, Fangyi Lin, Will Groves, Thomas Gonzalez, Nathan, Matthew are **interviewees / usability-test users** (quoted in the Trello cards and interview transcripts), NOT the team.

## Source legend
`[WHATSAPP]` team group chat | `[DISCORD #chan]` server channel | `[GIT]` commit | `[LINEAR]` issue opened | `[LINEAR-done]` issue completed | `[TRELLO]` board activity | `[MILESTONE]` course deadline | `[RESEARCH]` user-research event

## Which source feeds which document
- **HCD Portfolio (80%)** - everything here: the design-decision arc (WhatsApp/Discord/Linear/git), pivots and what was cut, the survey + interviews (Appendix), the Trello User-Need/Interaction/UI framing, and finding-to-change traceability.
- **Pitch Leaflet (10%)** - the origin/problem story (March-May WhatsApp ideation and brief selection), the value proposition, and target-audience discussion.
- **Copyright/Legal Report (10%)** - least served by chronology; rely on the codebase dependency tree (package.json files) plus any data/privacy/third-party threads (Clerk, hosting, GDPR) that surface below.

## Caveats / data gaps
- `drp-context/interviews/initial interviews/luke interview.txt` exported **EMPTY (0 bytes)** - transcript lost; recover from source if it still exists.
- Linear CSV carries issue **descriptions but no comments**; the Trello export has **no card comments**. Real decision threads therefore live in WhatsApp/Discord.
- Interview anchor dates are **approximate** (transcripts carry no timestamp); placed by iteration phase.
- Pure media / gif / embed / bare-URL posts are stripped - this is the substantive-text view, not a byte-for-byte dump.

- `12:06` `[WHATSAPP]` **Nelson Gong**: Idea A — Polymarket for friends Challenge (~60 words): "Friends constantly predict each other — who'll flake, who'll bail, who won't last the month — but it stays as group-chat noise with nothing tracked, so it sharpens no one and the group keeps losing sessions and members to the no-shows everyone already saw coming. The forecasting instinct and the appetite to bet on it are already there; there's just nowhere those calls are real or mean anything." HMW (~40 words): "How might we give a friend group a lightweight prediction market on its own behaviour — so the bets it already makes become real and calling things right is rewarded, making the group more reliable as a byproduct of something it already enjoys?" Slide line: "A prediction market for your friend group — the bets you already make, made real, so the group runs better." Idea B — Waze for walking Challenge (~65 words): "Google Maps and Citymapper optimise walking for speed and treat the pavement as uniform — they can't tell you a route is deafening, mobbed, unlit, or has a step where the map promised a ramp. The people who actually walk these streets know all of this, but there's nowhere to put it, so everyone relearns the same bad routes the hard way and those conditions stay invisible to the people who maintain the streets." HMW (~40 words): "How might we give pedestrians a Waze-style live, community-maintained layer for walking — routing around noise, crowding, safety and broken accessibility — so anyone, especially those for whom a bad route is a real barrier, moves with less friction, while operators finally see where the street fails?" Slide line: "Waze for walking — a live, crowd-maintained layer so the city's worst-kept routes stop being a surprise." One decision-relevant consequence, since you're mid-vote: this reframing widens the gap toward B, not narrows it. As "Waze for walking," B's only real weakness from before — hard recruitment of a niche user group — disappears: representative users are now anyone who walks London (trivially accessible), and the neurodivergent/wheelchair/night-safety angle becomes the wider-impact story the Milestone 4 rubric explicitly rewards, instead of being the access bottleneck. A as "Polymarket for friends" softens the gambling-punishment objection but it's still your own peer group, so the comfort-zone rubric penalty is unchanged. Worth raising with the two A-voters: the thing that made A's risk profile look comparable was my mis-framing, not the idea.

- `11:41` `[WHATSAPP]` **Nelson Gong**: Skeleton — bold lines are the things you must get to; everything underneath is a probe to use if the answer is thin, not a script.0. Warm-up (~2 min)  Who's in your main friend group? How do you typically keep in touch? (You're not collecting data here — you're getting them comfortable and gathering the cast of characters you can refer back to.) 1. The central excavation: the last failed plan (~8–10 min — spend the budget here)  "Tell me about the most recent time you tried to see friends and it didn't happen. Walk me through it from the first message." Probes — use what's needed, in any order:  Who proposed it? What exactly did they say? What did you reply, and why that? Was there a "maybe" or a soft response from anyone? What was the exact wording? When you saw that, what did you assume the person meant? What did you do next? What did others do next? When did you personally decide it wasn't happening — and what told you that? Was there a moment, or did it just drift? Did anyone ever say "no"? Or did it just go quiet? How did you feel about it afterwards? Did you bring it up later?   This single excavation tests cascade, silence-decay, and default-failure simultaneously. If you get nothing else from the interview, get this.2. The "maybe" mechanism (~4 min)  "When you send a 'maybe', what do you actually mean by it — in your head?" (probe for a percentage if they're up for it: "if you had to put a number on it — how likely?") "And when you receive a 'maybe' from a friend, what do you do with it?" (same probe — what % do they read it as?) The gap between these two answers is your evidence. If they show the asymmetry unprompted, that's quotable gold. 3. The polite veto (~4 min)  "Tell me about the last time someone suggested something you weren't keen on, but everyone else seemed into it. What did you actually say?" Probes:  What stopped you from saying you weren't keen? What did you think would happen if you did? Did the plan happen? Did you go? How was it?   Mirror: "And the last time you proposed something and got a lukewarm response from someone — what did you read into it?" 4. Initiator cost (~3 min)  "Who's usually the one who organises in your group?" Probes:  How does that feel for them? (or for you, if it's them) Have you ever wanted to suggest something but didn't actually send the message? What stopped you? The last time a plan worked out — who made it happen? What did they do?   5. Latency / risk-free intent — kill-or-confirm (~3 min)Without describing anything:  "Have you ever wanted to suggest meeting up but didn't? What stopped you?" That's it. Don't lead. If they volunteer "I didn't know if anyone else was free / would want to" unprompted, the private-bid mechanism has legs. If they say "I forgot / couldn't be bothered", a different design wins. Let the answer guide you, not the other way round.6. Wrap (~2 min)  Anything you wish worked differently about how your group makes plans? Anyone else in your group I should talk to? (snowball — your sample multiplier) Only now, if asked, you can briefly describe what you're exploring — never before.

- `12:51` `[DISCORD #resources]` **gong8**: NEW GOOGLE SLIDES, MILESTONE 1

- `12:46` `[LINEAR]` **gonglx8**: DRP-14 "Walking skeleton: persist moment loop in Postgres (Drizzle) + architecture diagram" [Done] — assignee gonglx8
  > For the M2 (Walking Skeleton) milestone the digital touchpoint must use a **database** for persistent state. Today the moment loop runs end-to-end RN → tRPC → API but with an **in-memory** store.
  > 
  > Scope:
  > 
  > * Replace the in-memory `moments` store with **Drizzle/Postgres** (`moments` + `responses` tables), keeping the thin scope (just the moment loop).
  > * Demo seeding: one proposal ("Dinner tonight? · Pho House · Thu 7pm", quorum 3) with two participants already IN, so the dev user's answer is decisive (Yes → clears 3/3, No → fizzles 2/3).
  > * Make `moments.mine` / `respond` / `resolve` DB-backed; keep the privacy shapes (no participant ids / tally leaked).
  > * Generate + apply the Drizzle migration; verify end-to-end against a live Postgres (Docker).
  > * Add a simple markdown **architecture diagram** (RN → tRPC → API → Postgres).
  > * Commit the walking skeleton + DB work properly.
  > 
  > Outcome: a genuine walking skeleton that links all architectural components incl. the DB — satisfies the M2 DB requirement.

- `14:04` `[LINEAR]` **gonglx8**: DRP-15 "BeThere: full-loop walking skeleton (suggest → availability → matching → moment → plan)" [Done] — assignee gonglx8
  > Extend the moment-only slice (PR #9, now on `dev`) into the full BeThere loop, partially functional and DB-backed, per the reference implementation spec.
  > 
  > ## Scope
  > 
  > * **Shared** (`@bethere/shared`): add `PartOfDay`, `Slot`, `CreateSuggestionInput`, `DropAvailabilityInput`; pure logic `quorum.ts`, `time.ts`, `matching.ts` (`findClearingSlot`), and `findLinchpins`; unit tests.
  > * **API** (`@bethere/api`): tables `users/groups/groupMembers/suggestions/availability/plans` + reshaped `moments`; `services/bethere.ts` (`tryFireMoment`, `resolveMoment`); routers `groups`/`suggestions`/`availability` + extended `moments` (`mine` with group members + `msLeft`, `plan` read); reseeded demo; clean baseline migration.
  > * **Mobile** (`@bethere/mobile`): `useState` route switcher + screens Home, Suggest, Availability, Floating, The Moment (real "I'm in if…" picker + countdown), Reveal.
  > 
  > ## Privacy invariants enforced
  > 
  > * Availability never returned except to its owner; no names/counts.
  > * `moments.mine` returns the *group's* members (for the conditional picker) — never the moment's participants or any tally.
  > * On clear, only the IN crowd is revealed; fizzle is silent.
  > 
  > ## Non-goals (this slice)
  > 
  > Fonts (Lora/Inter), push notifications, AI seeding, calendar, real auth, linchpin nudge delivery, websockets. Wired behind existing interfaces later.
  > 
  > Branch `feat/bethere-full-loop` → PR into `dev`.

- `14:30` `[LINEAR]` **gonglx8**: DRP-16 "Deploy API to a public live backend (AWS App Runner + RDS) with CD" [Done] — assignee gonglx8
  > Move the `@bethere/api` server off localhost onto a public, HTTPS backend the mobile app can connect to, so we satisfy the M2 rubric's "public deployment + functioning CD" software criterion.
  > 
  > **Target architecture (AWS, us-east-1):**
  > 
  > * API → **AWS App Runner** (free HTTPS on `*.awsapprunner.com`, autoscaling), container image from **ECR**.
  > * DB → **RDS Postgres 16** (`db.t4g.micro`), **private**, reached via an App Runner **VPC connector**.
  > * CD → GitHub Actions on push to `main`: build/push image to ECR → trigger App Runner deploy. Uses a **scoped IAM user** (never root keys).
  > 
  > **Phase 1 — code (commit to** `dev`**):**
  > 
  > * `db/client.ts`: enable SSL for non-local connections (RDS forces SSL).
  > * `index.ts`: run Drizzle migrations on boot; change `reseedDemo` → seed-only-if-empty (App Runner redeploys must not wipe data).
  > * `apps/api/package.json`: add `start` script; pin Node 20.
  > * `mobile/src/lib/trpc.ts`: read `EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL` (fallback localhost).
  > * New `apps/api/Dockerfile` + `.dockerignore`.
  > * Verify: typecheck, image builds, runs against local Postgres, `/trpc/health` 200, no drizzle schema/migration drift.
  > 
  > **Phase 2 — provision (captured in** `infra/`**):** ECR · RDS · VPC connector + SG rules · IAM ECR-access role · App Runner service.
  > 
  > **Phase 3 — connect + CD:** point mobile at live URL & verify; scoped IAM user → GitHub secrets → deploy workflow.
  > 
  > Account: 208569836255 · region us-east-1.

- `15:40` `[LINEAR]` **gonglx8**: DRP-19 "CD APK needs Metro — build release (standalone) not debug" [Done] — assignee gonglx8
  > The Android CD (`.github/workflows/cd.yml`) ran `./gradlew assembleDebug -PbundleInDebug=true`. There is no committed `android/` (it's generated by `expo prebuild` in CI), and that gradle property isn't read by Expo's generated build files, so it's a no-op. Result: a **debug APK with no embedded JS bundle**, which tries to load from Metro (`localhost:8081`) at runtime → on-device error "unable to load script…".
  > 
  > **Fix:** build a **release** APK. Verified locally (prebuild + inspect): the Expo SDK 56 / RN 0.85 template's `release` buildType uses `signingConfig signingConfigs.debug`, so `assembleRelease` produces an installable, debug-signed `app-release.apk`; Hermes is enabled so JS is embedded as bytecode. `EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL` is still inlined during the release bundle step.
  > 
  > Change `cd.yml`: `assembleRelease`; artifact name `app-release`; path `apps/mobile/android/app/build/outputs/apk/release/app-release.apk`.

  > - [ ] CreateEvent + Groups tab (list, GroupDetail CRUD, CreateGroup)

  > 
  > * New `apps/api/src/logger.ts`: singleton Pino, LOG_LEVEL (default info), pretty transport when NODE_ENV !== production, scope binding (boot/http/trpc/db).

- `17:39` `[LINEAR]` **gonglx8**: DRP-22 "Live API stuck on old schema: migrate-on-boot fails (type exists) → App Runner rollback" [Done] — assignee gonglx8
  > **Symptom:** Android APK shows "Couldn't reach the server." Network/APK are fine (URL baked in, INTERNET perm present, HTTPS cert valid). Root cause is server-side.
  > 
  > **Root cause (confirmed via App Runner application logs):** the concrete-event pivot reset the Drizzle migration baseline (new `0000_greedy_killmonger.sql`), but the live RDS still holds the OLD (suggestion/availability/moment) schema. On boot the new image runs migrate-on-boot → `CREATE TYPE "response_kind"` → `error: type "response_kind" already exists` (code 42710) → process crashes → health check fails → **App Runner rolls back to the old image**. So the live API keeps serving the old model: `events.mine`→404 (app calls this), while `availability.mine`/`moments.mine`→200. Every screen's blanket `.catch(()=>setError(true))` surfaces this as "Couldn't reach the server."
  > 
  > This is the "reset the migration baseline → must reset the DB too" gotcha from CLAUDE.md.
  > 
  > **Fix:** reset the live RDS schema (throwaway demo data only — no real user data) so the new baseline applies cleanly, then migrate-on-boot + `seedDemoIfEmpty` repopulate. Then redeploy the current concrete-event image and verify `events.mine`→200.
  > 
  > **Follow-up:** make migrate-on-boot resilient to a fresh-baseline-vs-existing-DB drift (or document a reset runbook) so this doesn't recur on the next baseline reset.

- `18:00` `[LINEAR]` **gonglx8**: DRP-23 "Optimise CI/CD pipeline (Gradle + Docker caching, paths filter, concurrency)" [Done]
  > Four targeted optimisations to the GitHub Actions pipeline:
  > 
  > 1. **Gradle caching** in the Android APK build (`cd.yml`) via `gradle/actions/setup-gradle` - the slowest job currently rebuilds Gradle state from cold every run.
  > 2. **Docker build cache** for the API image (`deploy-api.yml`) via `docker/build-push-action` with `type=gha` layer cache, plus a BuildKit pnpm-store cache mount in the API `Dockerfile`.
  > 3. `paths:` **filter +** `workflow_dispatch` on the APK build so an API-only push to `main` no longer triggers a full Android build, and APKs can still be cut manually.
  > 4. `concurrency` **groups** on all three workflows so superseded runs are cancelled (CI + APK) / queued safely (deploy).
  > 
  > Branch: dev.
  > 
  > ---
  > 
  > **Done** in commit `57873c9` on `dev` ("perf(ci): cache Gradle + Docker layers, scope APK build, add concurrency"). YAML validated; cache benefit observable on the second-and-later real runs. Will land in prod on the next `dev` -> `main` merge.

- `13:12` `[LINEAR]` **gonglx8**: DRP-24 "Clerk auth (Google OAuth) + Vercel-hosted web target" [Done]
  > Add Clerk (Google OAuth) authentication and a Vercel-hostable web build to BeThere from one Expo codebase.
  > 
  > **Approach**
  > 
  > * One Expo app renders the same screens on native + web (react-native-web). No `apps/web`, no duplicated screens.
  > * Clerk Google sign-in on both targets via `@clerk/clerk-expo`.
  > * API verifies Clerk session tokens networklessly (`@clerk/backend`), resolving a nullable `userId`; `protectedProcedure` enforces auth while `/trpc/health` stays public (App Runner health check).
  > * `DEV_AUTH_BYPASS` falls back to the `x-user-id` stub; the sideloaded CD APK ships a flag-gated "Continue as test user" button that signs in as seed user `u_dev`.
  > 
  > **Docs**
  > 
  > * Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-06-01-clerk-auth-and-web-target-design.md`
  > * Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-06-01-clerk-auth-and-web-target.md`
  > 
  > **Tasks**: B1-B6 (backend auth), C1-C7 (client + web), D1-D2 (CD + manual ops), V1 (verification). Working on `dev`.

- `14:33` `[LINEAR]` **gonglx8**: DRP-25 "UX overhaul: refined-neobrutalist visual system (Peach to Lavender)" [Done] (HCD, Improvement)
  > Restyle the whole mobile app from the current flat "Sage" identity to a **refined neobrutalist** visual system, chosen through a brainstorming session.
  > 
  > **Look:** peach-to-lavender gradient background, white boxy cards with 2px black outlines and hard offset shadows, heavy Archivo display headings, monospace (Space Mono) date chips, tilted pink "sticker" tags, restrained two-accent palette (pink = brand/urgent, green = going).
  > 
  > This is a **restyle, not a rebuild.** No product behaviour, tRPC contracts, navigation graph, or privacy model changes. New tokens go in `apps/mobile/src/theme.ts`.
  > 
  > **Design spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-06-01-ux-overhaul-visual-system-design.md`
  > **Mockup (open in browser):** `docs/mockups/m3-ux-overhaul/all-screens.html`
  > 
  > **Key tokens**
  > 
  > * Gradient `#FCEFE8` to `#ECEAFF`; surface `#FFFFFF`; ink `#111`; muted `#7D7A86`
  > * Pink (brand/urgent) `#FF5CA8`; green (going) `#34A853`
  > * Fonts: Archivo (display), Inter (UI), Space Mono (chips)
  > * Cards 18px radius, 2px borders, hard shadow `4px 4px 0 #111`
  > 
  > **Scope (per screen)**
  > 
  > - [ ] Theme: tokens (colour, type, radius, border, shadow) + load Archivo/Inter/Space Mono fonts + add `expo-linear-gradient`
  > - [ ] Reusable primitives: ScreenBackground (gradient), HardShadow wrapper, Card, Button (pink/green/outline), Chip, Field, Tabs, DateChip, StickerTag, StatusCheck, Avatar, BottomSheet, BackBar
  > - [ ] Home (featured meet + status tabs + checklist)
  > - [ ] Event detail (RSVP: in / I'll go if... / can't)
  > - [ ] "I'll go if..." bottom sheet (toggle + member picker)
  > - [ ] Suggest a meet (create event form)
  > - [ ] Your groups (list)
  > - [ ] Group detail (members + add)
  > - [ ] New group
  > 
  > **Open questions**
  > 
  > * Navigation: keep restyled bottom tabs (recommended) vs top switch
  > * Keep two accents (pink + green) vs single pink
  > * Confirm home redesign (featured + tabs + checklist) replaces status-sectioned dashboard
  > 
  > **Acceptance**
  > 
  > * All seven screens restyled to the spec; tokens centralised in `theme.ts` (no scattered literals)
  > * `pnpm lint`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm test` green; no em dashes; data flow + privacy intact

- `18:23` `[LINEAR]` **gonglx8**: DRP-28 "Local dev: Clerk sign-in 401s every request because API has no CLERK_JWT_KEY" [Done]
  > ## Symptom
  > 
  > Signed in via Google (Clerk) on the local app, every tRPC call returns 401 UNAUTHORIZED (`user=anon`), the dashboard shows "Couldn't reach the server", and the new gating falls back to "Create a group". API logs: `[auth] bearer token present but did not verify` repeatedly.
  > 
  > ## Root cause
  > 
  > The mobile app sends a real Clerk JWT (`Authorization: Bearer ...`). The local `apps/api/.env` has no `CLERK_JWT_KEY`, so `verifyClerkToken` throws and `resolveAuth` degrades to unauthenticated. `DEV_AUTH_BYPASS=1` did not help because `resolveAuth` only fell back to the `x-user-id` stub when NO bearer token was present - and the Clerk client always attaches one.
  > 
  > ## Fix
  > 
  > Added a `clerkConfigured` input to `resolveAuth` (set from `!!process.env.CLERK_JWT_KEY`). When Clerk verification is not even possible (key unset, i.e. local dev) and `DEV_AUTH_BYPASS` is on, an attached but unverifiable bearer token is ignored and the request falls back to the `u_dev` seed user instead of 401ing. Prod is unaffected: App Runner sets `CLERK_JWT_KEY`, so real tokens still verify and bad ones still 401.
  > 
  > Verified: 8/8 auth unit tests pass; live curl with a fake bearer token now returns 200 with u_dev's seed groups.
- `18:23` `[LINEAR-done]`: DRP-28 "Local dev: Clerk sign-in 401s every request because API has no CLERK_JWT_KEY" completed

- `18:36` `[LINEAR]` **gonglx8**: DRP-29 "M3: Convergence model revamp (react-to-options -> blind timed moment)" [Done]
  > Experimental M3 iteration on branch `feat/m3-convergence-model`. Unifies the M2 concrete-event model with the archived loose-availability ideas into ONE plan whose `when` is expressed with variable precision; the precision silently routes behaviour (no user-facing "type").
  > 
  > Design discussion + approved plan: `docs/superpowers/specs/` + `/Users/gong/.claude/plans/ultrathink-we-want-to-rippling-prism.md`.
  > 
  > Core flow:
  > 
  > * CREATE: what + who + WHEN (one exact time | a few specific times | timescale + day-part band).
  > * REACT: people tap which candidate times work (cheap feasibility, no availability grid).
  > * LOCK: creator sees tally, taps "lock it" on the winning slot.
  > * MOMENT: blind + timed commitment (in / in-if-\[people\] / can't; no "maybe"; blind until resolved). Reuses resolveIn + revealGoing.
  > * OUTCOME: cleared -> reveal who's in; under-quorum & contingent (fuzzy/options) -> silent fizzle. Exact/committed plans always happen.
  > 
  > Decisions locked:
  > 
  > * Pool size IS the type (1 / few / coarse-window), invisible to user; backend branches.
  > * Convergence = react-to-candidates, NOT availability grid.
  > * Moment trigger = creator-fired after seeing tally (auto-fire deferred).
  > * Real-time = \~5s polling + local notification taste; real remote push = tech debt (needs dev build + device + Apple acct; impossible on iOS Simulator, removed from Expo Go SDK 53+).
  > * A/B test: M2 model stays on `main` (version A); this branch is version B; localhost is fine for sessions.
  > 
  > Goal: polished prototype of all three pools by Fri 5 Jun (M3), runnable alongside M2 for real-user A/B testing.

- `12:05` `[DISCORD #general]` **gong8**: ```echo "===== Lukas network diagnostic ====="
  > echo "--- 1. Wi-Fi SSID + signal ---"
  > nmcli -t -f active,ssid,bssid,chan,freq dev wifi 2>/dev/null | grep '^yes' || iwgetid 2>/dev/null
  > echo
  > echo "--- 2. IP address + netmask (the big one: /24 vs /32?) ---"
  > ip -br addr show | grep -v 'lo '
  > echo
  > echo "--- 3. Detailed addr on the wifi iface ---"
  > WIFI=$(nmcli -t -f device,type dev 2>/dev/null | awk -F: '$2=="wifi"{print $1; exit}')
  > echo "wifi iface: ${WIFI:-unknown}"
  > ip addr show "${WIFI:-wlan0}" 2>/dev/null | grep -E "inet |link/ether"
  > echo
  > echo "--- 4. Default gateway + routes ---"
  > ip route
  > echo
  > echo "--- 5. DHCP lease info (what the network actually handed out) ---"
  > nmcli dev show "${WIFI:-wlan0}" 2>/dev/null | grep -iE "IP4.ADDRESS|IP4.GATEWAY|IP4.DNS|DHCP4.OPTION.*(subnet|router|ip_address|broadcast)" | head -25
  > echo
  > echo "--- 6. Can he reach the gateway + internet? ---"
  > GW=$(ip route | awk '/default/{print $3; exit}')
  > echo "gateway: $GW"
  > ping -c 2 -W 2 "$GW" 2>&1 | tail -2
  > ping -c 2 -W 2 1.1.1.1 2>&1 | tail -2
  > echo
  > echo "--- 7. His phone/devices on same subnet? (ARP neighbours) ---"
  > ip neigh show | head
  > echo "===== end ====="```

- `20:36` `[LINEAR]` **gonglx8**: DRP-30 "Iteration 4: Float an idea (anonymous, collaborative, open-ended floating)" [Done]
  > M3 iteration-4 feature, from the three iteration interviews (Felicity / Tom / Luca+Nathan). Root problem they all hit: starting a plan carries social risk ("no one wants to say yes first"), which kills meetups before they exist. This attacks the front of the funnel.
  > 
  > **What ships (one feat/ branch, feat/float-an-idea -> PR into dev):**
  > 
  > * A "Float an idea": any member floats a loose idea, always unsigned (no names shown) and ownerless. Two collaborative chip axes - IDEA (fused what+where) and TIME (loose bands). Any member adds chips and one-tap +1. Exact +1 counts visible (momentum), identities hidden.
  > * A +1 is interest, not commitment. Deadline-only convergence (lazy-settle + staleness clamp). Hybrid tip: clear idea+time (2+ backers on a band) -> blind moment; hot idea, unresolved time -> short collecting round; cold -> fizzle.
  > * Create-flow overhaul: "+ New" -> "how pinned down?" dial (Float / Rough / Set), focused multi-step wizard (merges options+fuzzy into Rough, readable weekdays, drops quick-pick presets). Dashboard "Brewing" zone.
  > * Push assumed (the only blocker is the GBP99 Apple licence) - notification flows built on the local scaffold, faked, behind a seam for real push.
  > 
  > **Architecture:** reuse the events table (new "floating" phase + isAnonymous flag) rather than parallel tables - cleaner anonymity hand-off. reconcileFloat is a pure function so AWS Bedrock can later replace it (smart reconciliation / sparse-float seeding).
  > 
  > **Accepted risks (MVP):** small-group count leak (honest "no names" copy + min-heat>=2), free-text chip fragmentation (client dedup; semantic = future Bedrock), anonymous spam (createdByUserId kept server-side for accountability).
  > 
  > **Deferred:** heat-based early tipping, Bedrock reconciler/seeder, light chat integration, semantic chip dedup.
  > 
  > Plan doc: /Users/gong/.claude/plans/docs-drp-context-interviews-m3-intervie-delegated-newt.md

- `13:32` `[LINEAR]` **gonglx8**: DRP-31 "Isolated dev deployment (web + backend) mirroring main" [Done]
  > Stand up a second, isolated deployment for the `dev` branch - web frontend (Vercel Preview) + backend (AWS App Runner) + its own RDS Postgres - mirroring the prod (`main`) stack, so in-flight work can be exercised at a stable URL without touching prod.
  > 
  > ## Why isolation (not dev web -> prod backend)
  > 
  > The web build bakes `EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL` at build time, and `dev` is currently 8 commits ahead of `main` with a new migration (`0004_parched_rage.sql`) and changed API contract (new `routers/floats.ts`, `schema.ts`, `packages/shared`). Dev's frontend would call procedures/columns prod doesn't have yet and break. So dev needs its own backend + DB.
  > 
  > ## Plan
  > 
  > Mirror the prod AWS stack (set up originally by `infra/aws-deploy.sh`).
  > 
  > **Reused (no new resource):** default VPC + subnets, ECR repo `bethere-api` (separate `:dev` tag), IAM pull role `bethere-apprunner-ecr-role`, VPC connector `bethere-vpc-conn`, RDS SG `bethere-rds-sg`.
  > 
  > **New AWS resources (\~$15-25/mo):**
  > 
  > * RDS `bethere-db-dev` (db.t4g.micro, pg 16.14, SG bethere-rds-sg, subnet group bethere-subnets)
  > * App Runner `bethere-api-dev` (image bethere-api:dev, 0.5vCPU/1GB, env mirrors prod with dev DATABASE_URL + fresh ADMIN_RESET_TOKEN)
  > 
  > **Repo changes (to** `dev`**):**
  > 
  > * `vercel.json` - un-gate `dev` in ignoreCommand
  > * `.github/workflows/deploy-api-dev.yml` - push `:dev` tag on push to dev
  > * `infra/aws-deploy-dev.sh` - reproducible provisioning record
  > * `docs/runbook-deploy.md` - dev stack section
  > * Broaden `bethere-ci` IAM policy to `service/bethere-api*/*`
  > 
  > **Vercel:** Preview-scoped env (EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL -> dev API, CLERK publishable key, DEV_AUTH=1).
  > 
  > ## Verification
  > 
  > * `curl https://<dev-url>/trpc/health` -> ok; a `floats.*` proc responds (proves :dev image)
  > * dev DB isolated from prod; prod web + API untouched
  > * push to dev triggers deploy-api-dev.yml; Vercel dev Preview loads against dev API

- `13:49` `[LINEAR]` **gonglx8**: DRP-32 "Lock-in deadline + bounded additions for flexible plans" [Done]
  > Make the lock-in deadline logically correct and good UX for non-exact (options/fuzzy) plans, and bound member-added times.
  > 
  > DONE on `dev` (6 commits, 332f06b..cd10d8d). Spec: docs/superpowers/specs/2026-06-03-lockin-deadline-bounded-additions-design.md; plan: docs/superpowers/plans/2026-06-03-lockin-deadline-bounded-additions.md.
  > 
  > What shipped:
  > 
  > * Two intent-named defaults in packages/shared/src/logic/lock.ts: defaultLockAtForOptions (lead = clamp(T/3, MOMENT, 1 day) before earliest) and defaultLockAtForWindow (react = clamp(span/3, 2h, 3 days) from now), replacing the single naive defaultLockAt.
  > * addCandidateHorizon helper bounding member-added times (fuzzy: window's last slot; options: latest + min(span, 2 days)).
  > * events router: per-mode create default, override bound tightened to anchor - moment, bounded addCandidate (+ empty-spread guard), settleFloating anchored to last slot.
  > * floats router: tip default anchored to window's last slot.
  > * mobile: mirrored defaultLockAtForOptions + addCandidateHorizon; add-time picker bounded \[lockAt, horizon\] via new maximumDate support threaded through DateTimeField/Pill.
  > 
  > Verification: lint clean (92 files), typecheck clean (3 pkgs), tests pass (shared 44, api 14, mobile 5). Note: aggregate `pnpm test` does not self-exit due to a pre-existing mobile jest open-handle issue (tests pass, jest just does not terminate) - run per-package.
  > 
  > Out of scope (follow-up): moment duration relative to the event - the blind moment still opens at lockAt and runs \~1h regardless of how far the event is.

- `15:14` `[LINEAR]` **gonglx8**: DRP-40 "Moment duration should scale with how far away the event is" [Canceled]
  > Follow-up from [DRP-32](https://linear.app/drp-02/issue/DRP-32/lock-in-deadline-bounded-additions-for-flexible-plans) (lock-in deadline work). Out of scope there; needs design thought.
  > 
  > ## Problem
  > 
  > The blind "moment" opens at `lockAt` and runs a fixed \~1h (`momentEnd = min(lockTime + DEFAULT_MOMENT_MINUTES, chosenStartsAt)`), regardless of how far in the future the chosen event is. [DRP-32](https://linear.app/drp-02/issue/DRP-32/lock-in-deadline-bounded-additions-for-flexible-plans) made the lock-in deadline sensible, but the moment timing is still decoupled from the event:
  > 
  > * On a fuzzy/options plan whose winning slot is several days out, the lock can fire days before the event, so the blind, time-pressured RSVP moment opens \~1h that far ahead. The "decide now, it's soon" pressure that makes a moment a moment evaporates.
  > * Conversely the 1h floor can feel too short for a near-term plan, or mismatched when the chosen time is only a little after lock.
  > 
  > So the lock-in placement is now correct, but "when does the moment run, and for how long" is the remaining half of the same question.
  > 
  > ## Things to think about (not decided)
  > 
  > * Should the moment open at `lockAt` at all, or closer to the event (e.g. a fixed lead before `chosenStartsAt`)?
  > * Should moment duration scale with distance-to-event, with a floor/cap?
  > * Interaction with exact plans (which already open the moment at creation and run until the event).
  > * Keep it predictable / explainable to users - same bar we held in [DRP-32](https://linear.app/drp-02/issue/DRP-32/lock-in-deadline-bounded-additions-for-flexible-plans).
  > 
  > ## Pointers
  > 
  > * `apps/api/src/routers/events.ts`: `settleCollecting` (opens the moment on lock), `momentEnd` helper, `DEFAULT_MOMENT_MINUTES`, the exact-plan path in `create`.
  > * Design doc for context: docs/superpowers/specs/2026-06-03-lockin-deadline-bounded-additions-design.md (see its Non-goals section).

- `12:54` `[LINEAR]` **gonglx8**: DRP-41 "Unified "suggest a hangout" flow: merge float/flexible/concrete into one votable plan" [Done] — assignee gonglx8
  > Redesign the meetup-creation workflow. Replaces the create-flow parts of the convergence model ([DRP-29](https://linear.app/drp-02/issue/DRP-29/m3-convergence-model-revamp-react-to-options-blind-timed-moment)) and the standalone float feature ([DRP-30](https://linear.app/drp-02/issue/DRP-30/iteration-4-float-an-idea-anonymous-collaborative-open-ended-floating)).
  > 
  > ## Why
  > 
  > The create dial sorts by time-precision (float/rough/set), but users think in intent and start loose then harden. Luca could not map "an afternoon meetup, just see who's free and who's down to do what" onto any of the three. Jargon ("auto-tips", "spark", "brewing") compounds it. Grounded in all 7 interviews + a competitive scan.
  > 
  > ## What
  > 
  > One create flow, one votable plan, one board:
  > 
  > * A plan owns two collaborative candidate lists - **time** and **what/where** - each with **public +1 counts, names never shown**.
  > * Two creator checkboxes, **off by default** (group can shape it): **Lock the times**, **Lock the plan**. The old three modes become positions of these two flags + how many times exist.
  > * "auto-tips"/tipAt -> editable **"Decides by"**; tip-vs-lock vocabulary unified to one.
  > * **Float frontend nuked**; the evolved voting board is the only board.
  > * **Anonymity always on**, clearly communicated on the frontend this iteration.
  > * Principle: public for momentum (votes), blind for honesty (the who's-in moment).
  > 
  > ## Spec
  > 
  > Design spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-06-04-unified-suggest-flow-design.md` (includes the full change inventory: deletions, merges, generalizations, renames, DB reshape + migration, risks, and a foundation-first sequencing).
  > 
  > ## Status
  > 
  > Design approved in brainstorm. Spec committed and pending team review before the implementation plan. Large refactor - to be landed on a `feat/*` branch, PR into `dev`, with frontend/backend/shared changes in one PR (trpc type-chain coupling).

- `16:45` `[LINEAR]` **gonglx8**: DRP-42 "Redo: start a meetup from a previous one" [Done] — assignee gonglx8
  > Kill re-creation friction for groups that meet regularly (the "weekly DnD" case). Let a creator start a new plan FROM a past one instead of rebuilding it.
  > 
  > ## Scope (wizard-only + one read query)
  > 
  > * After group selection in `CreateWizard`, insert a conditional **source** step: **Start fresh** vs **Use a previous meetup** (pickable list).
  > * Shown ONLY if the chosen group has >= 1 past (cleared) meetup; otherwise skipped, wizard unchanged.
  > * Picking a previous meetup pre-fills **activities, lock settings (lockTimes/lockThings), location, notes**. NOT the time (always stale, left blank) and NOT title (server resolves from winning activity as today). No RSVP/roster carry-over.
  > * Both choices flow through the normal steps; the clone reuses the existing `events.create` mutation (no new create path).
  > * One new read-only procedure: `events.pastForGroup({ groupId })` returning each cleared plan's clonable shell. Member-scoped, no creator identity, no RSVP data.
  > 
  > ## Explicitly deferred / non-goals
  > 
  > * Automated recurring meetups (a self-spawning series). Redo covers the weekly case in a few taps; no scheduler exists by design.
  > * Roster / RSVP carry-over (each redo is a fresh honest round).
  > * Per-group history surface or dashboard "redo" buttons (redo lives only in the wizard).
  > 
  > ## Design spec
  > 
  > `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-06-04-redo-from-previous-meetup-design.md` (commit 7fa03c4).
  > 
  > Builds on the unified suggest flow ([DRP-41](https://linear.app/drp-02/issue/DRP-41/unified-suggest-a-hangout-flow-merge-floatflexibleconcrete-into-one)).
  > 
  > ## Sequencing
  > 
  > 1. `@bethere/shared`: add the `PastForGroup` output schema.
  > 2. `apps/api`: add `events.pastForGroup` + tests.
  > 3. `apps/mobile`: conditional source step + pre-fill wiring in `CreateWizard` + tests.
  > 
  > All land together on `dev` (tRPC type chain couples them).

- `17:30` `[LINEAR]` **gonglx8**: DRP-43 "UX fixes: create-flow lock placement, editable meetups, typography, countdown" [Done]
  > Five UX complaints against the unified suggest flow, addressed as one cohesive pass. Design spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-06-04-ux-fixes-design.md`.
  > 
  > 1. **Create flow** - move both lock toggles off the bloated "A few options" step onto the Times and Activities steps they govern; add an optional Title field at create (still auto-derived if blank).
  > 2. **Editable meetups** - any group member can edit title/location/notes after creation, before the plan clears. Concurrency-safe via per-field compare-and-set (CAS): no lost updates, no false conflicts, no schema migration. Inline edit bottom sheet on EventDetail.
  > 3. **Typography** - remove SpaceMono everywhere (e.g. "4 on the table"); map to Archivo display / Inter bold with tabular-nums.
  > 4. **Countdown** - delete the green DrainBar; replace with a prominent plain-language "Voting closes in 2 days" / "RSVP closes in 5 hours" line, urgency via pink text under 1 hour.
  > 
  > Out of scope: realtime sync, editing votes/responses, structural edits (times/activities/locks/deadlines), edit attribution.

- `20:34` `[LINEAR]` **gonglx8**: DRP-44 "Polish + unify mobile UI (dashboard, voting, detail, nav, wizard)" [Done] — assignee gonglx8
  > A cohesive polish + unification pass on the Expo mobile app, addressing a full punch-list of UX/consistency complaints and unifying as many components as possible into one shared vocabulary. Work lands on branch `feat/redo-from-previous-meetup` (per direction to stay on it). Design spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-06-04-mobile-polish-unify-design.md`.
  > 
  > ## Decisions
  > 
  > * Dashboard: pinned pink "N ACTION(S) REQUIRED" panel + colour-coded **Going / Open / Done** segmented filter (default Going).
  > * Detail top: meetup title in the back bar; group drops to a hero sub-line.
  > 
  > ## Punch-list (all must be addressed)
  > 
  >  1. Voting screen ugly; add-activity vs add-time inconsistent -> one `AddComposer`.
  >  2. Add-time Add/Cancel not in theme -> themed mini-buttons.
  >  3. Detail top shows only the group (arbitrary) -> meetup title in bar.
  >  4. Dashboard "chopped" -> one card system, contained action panel.
  >  5. Bottom bar: highlight active tab (filled pill, visible boundaries).
  >  6. Voting / live-RSVP / after meetup screens inconsistent -> one hero shell + rhythm.
  >  7. Badge slop ("weigh in", "you've had your say") -> terse `StatusPill`.
  >  8. "Action required" + count -> "N ACTION(S) REQUIRED".
  >  9. Account -> top-right avatar circle on Meetups + Groups; drop Account tab.
  > 10. Filter ugly -> coloured segmented control, default Going.
  > 11. Awaiting bug: cleared/past/unvoted shown in Awaiting -> routed to Done.
  > 12. Sort by time; past harder to show.
  > 13. Kill "3 on the table" everywhere.
  > 14. Time-left must be obvious/scary -> one big `Countdown`.
  > 15. Reduce slop copy globally.
  > 16. Going shows upcoming; truly past separated into Done.
  > 17. Event card: "3 going" text only, remove face pile.
  > 18. Unify inconsistent shared components (the big theme).
  > 19. Wizard: split location/notes from deadlines into separate steps.
  > 
  > ## Unification targets
  > 
  > One header (`ScreenHeader` replacing Heading+BackBar), one segmented control (`Segmented` replacing Tabs+Toggle), one add affordance (`AddComposer`), one `Countdown`, one small-pill family (`Pill`/`StatusPill`/DateChip), one checkbox (`SelectCheck` everywhere; kill bespoke CheckRow box), shared status predicates (`lib/status.ts`), Button `sm`+`ghost`.
  > 
  > ## Verification
  > 
  > `pnpm lint` + `pnpm typecheck` + `pnpm test` green; adversarial multi-agent review of categorization correctness + unification completeness + punch-list coverage before done.

- `12:06` `[DISCORD #general]` **the_jlh**: The wizard is well received
  > "I love this. I love this a lot. You're like the wizard. 100%. Oh my God, it makes my life so much easier. I'm like, yes, this is what I want."
  > Too much text on one screen
  > "I can't lie, that's a lot of text. I'm not reading all that. Nelson, I think this is too much text. Like, no name shown is enough."
  > Navigation — too many taps
  > "What people like the most about apps is being able to get from one place to another without having to go through too many ticks. You don't want to have to go through like four. You don't want to just like button, button, button."
  > The CTA button copy needs to be snappier
  > "Say you're in instead of say if you're in. It's too long. It needs to be snappier."
  > Calendar/schedule page suggestion
  > "What you need is a calendar in this app. A calendar with like, suggested days, and then what I've suggested. So you know where you're going."
  > Combining groups UI is confusing
  > "What does combined mean?... you should just be able to like add each group separately to the event."
  > Location field needs clearer optional labelling
  > "There's no distinction between the two words... make the location a tiny bit bigger, make optional a tiny bit smaller."
  > Colour/progress bar is counterintuitive
  > "It does seem a bit counterintuitive to me that it's greener as you get closer and you need to reply... it makes you want to feel like you're nearly there, or that it's okay, you don't need to."
- `12:08` `[GIT]` **Leixin Gong**: docs(summary): massive spec-driven test overhaul
- `12:08` `[LINEAR]` **gonglx8**: DRP-45 "Bottom sheet not keyboard-safe: keyboard covers Edit details fields/Save" [Done]
  > On mobile, opening the on-screen keyboard while editing a meetup (the "Edit details" bottom sheet) covered the lower fields (Notes) and the Save button, since the sheet content was hidden behind the keyboard.
  > 
  > Root cause: the edit form (and the conditional people-picker) render inside `BottomSheet`, a `Modal` pinned to `flex-end`. React Native does not move modal content for the keyboard automatically.
  > 
  > Fix: wrap the sheet container in a `KeyboardAvoidingView` (`behavior: "padding"` on iOS, matching the existing `ScreenScroll` convention) so `flex-end` lifts the sheet above the keyboard; the scrim still fills behind it. Fixes every `BottomSheet` consumer at once.
  > 
  > Commit: d885e0f on `dev`. Verified: typecheck, biome, and the full mobile test suite (165 passing).
- `12:08` `[LINEAR-done]`: DRP-45 "Bottom sheet not keyboard-safe: keyboard covers Edit details fields/Save" completed

- `12:10` `[LINEAR]` **gonglx8**: DRP-46 "Spec-driven test suite + CI Postgres gate" [Done] — assignee gonglx8
  > Retroactive ticket for the test infrastructure + suite shipped on `feat/redo-from-previous-meetup` and merged to `dev` (PR #40). Tracked here so the board reflects it (it is one of the strongest "stable product / architecture" artifacts for the M3 review).
  > 
  > ## What
  > 
  > * DB-backed tRPC `createCaller` integration harness with a per-process throwaway Postgres, making the whole `events.ts` plan lifecycle testable for the first time.
  > * React Native render + tRPC-mock harness for mobile screens.
  > * \~566 spec-derived tests (derived from ARCHITECTURE.md / CLAUDE.md, not mirrored from the code), which caught and fixed 2 real bugs (create + addCandidate minute-dedup).
  > * Fixed the mobile jest non-exit hang (`--forceExit`).
  > * CI: added a postgres:16 service to node.js.yml and a new dev-check.yml gate (lint + typecheck + test on push/PR to `dev`).
  > 
  > End state: `pnpm check` is green (lint + typecheck + 521 tests + quality).
  > 
  > ## Refs
  > 
  > Commits 537c9b1, 380f921, fa13337, 67eb69e, 72129c6, f647ea1. Summary: docs/summary/2026-06-05-1206-massive-spec-driven-test-overhaul.md

  > Pre-M3-review work from a full multi-lens codebase critique (2026-06-05). The codebase is green (`pnpm check` passes: lint + typecheck + 521 tests + quality); these are demo polish, interview-driven UX, and small robustness fixes, not firefighting.

  > ## P0 - before the review
  >

  > - [ ] Seed a "Going" plan for u_dev so the default Dashboard tab is not empty (cleared plan with a future startsAt + a u_dev "yes").
  > - [ ] Soften the conditional "Go if \[people\]" copy + default to "At least one" (felicity could not name friends on camera, even privately).
  > - [ ] Bottom-tab inactive label contrast: muted -> ink (App.tsx:107).

  > ## P1 - polish week (correctness)
  > 
  > - [ ] Settle-on-write: respond/unrespond reject after momentEndsAt; toggleReaction/addCandidate/setOptOut reject after decidesBy (events.ts).
  > - [ ] SEED_ON_BOOT fail-safe default (if-empty, not the destructive reset) (index.ts:102).
  > - [ ] GroupDetail self-remove dead-end: add `me` to groups.get, hide self remove.
  > - [ ] Moment answer buttons guard when liveMsLeft <= 0 (EventDetail).
  > - [ ] RespondInput stray cond (bug #3) server strip + un-skip test.
  > - [ ] Future-time validation on create/lock (defense in depth).
  > 
  > ## P1 - polish week (UX / rubric)

  > - [ ] Relative / quick-preset times on the create times step (reuse the already-built shared expandWindow).
  > - [ ] Surface the thesis (anonymity / momentum / no-organiser) in SignIn tagline + vote-board + moment copy.
  > - [ ] First-run onboarding / "what is a group?" empty-state explainers.
  > - [ ] 12h vs 24h time-format consistency.
  > - [ ] a11y labels on the shared ui/ primitives.
  > 
  > ## P2 - defer past deadline
  > 
  > duration/end-time (Notes already covers "park 12-3") · location-as-place (Maps Linking) · delete dead window.ts band subsystem · durable lock.ts de-dup · web DateTimeField weekday (only if web is graded).

- `13:18` `[LINEAR]` **gonglx8**: DRP-48 "Fix 36 bugs from full-project code review (parallel bug hunt)" [Done]
  > Whole-project bug hunt (multi-agent code review) surfaced 36 verified findings (30 CONFIRMED, 6 PLAUSIBLE; 35 bugs + 1 cleanup). Fixing all via file-owned parallel lanes on `dev`, committed in modular chunks. This issue tracks the batch.
  > 
  > ## High
  > 
  > * `apps/api/src/routers/groups.ts` removeMember: no last-member guard -> removing the final member permanently orphans the group.
  > * `apps/api/src/routers/events.ts` respond/unrespond/addCandidate/toggleReaction/lock: gate on stale stored phase (loadEvent never settles) -> writes accepted after the moment/decides-by deadline (blind-window violation).
  > 
  > ## Medium (server)
  > 
  > * events.ts lock UPDATE has no phase guard in WHERE -> double-lock clobbers the moment window.
  > * events.ts lock bypasses the post-deadline fizzle rule (two settle paths disagree).
  > * events.ts toggleReaction +1 insert lacks onConflictDoNothing -> concurrent +1 hits PK -> 500.
  > * events.ts create does not trim/dedupe activity candidates -> defeats both-axes-pinned moment shortcut.
  > * events.ts create accepts past startsAt -> plan born already-expired, silent self-fizzle.
  > * groups.ts removeMember leaves the removed user's reactions/responses/opt-outs (keep counting toward tally/quorum/reveal).
  > * groups.ts addMember: no user-existence check -> FK violation surfaced as 500.
  > * index.ts RATE_LIMIT_MAX / RATE_LIMIT_WINDOW / PORT: `??`-vs-empty-string trap (outage / random port).
  > * shared/schemas.ts startsAt/decidesBy/replyBy bare z.string() -> malformed timestamps cross the boundary.
  > 
  > ## Medium (mobile)
  > 
  > * lib/status.ts moment branch missing declined guard -> opted-out members falsely nagged.
  > * EventDetail.tsx CAS conflict does not refresh baseline -> spurious save-again loop.
  > * CreateWizard.tsx timeIsos not minute-deduped -> preview diverges from server.
  > * CreateWizard.tsx stale decidesInvalid permanently disables Send.
  > * GroupDetail.tsx error flag never reset; unsaved rename clobbered on reload.
  > * ui/DateTimeField.tsx seed ignores min/max -> out-of-bounds dismiss-commit.
  > 
  > ## Low / Plausible / Cleanup
  > 
  > * events.ts whitespace/duplicate activity labels; toggleReaction non-atomic +/opt-out.
  > * CreateWizard.tsx decidesInvalid missing lower bound; no activity cap (>10 -> generic error).
  > * EventDetail.tsx partial CAS save does not reload.
  > * admin/reset-auth.ts byte-length vs code-unit-length -> RangeError -> 500.
  > * db/schema.ts responses missing unique(event_id,user_id) and FK on user_id (+ migration).
  > * notifications.ts moment-open ping cancelled-not-rescheduled.
  > * lib/auth.ts empty-string firstName kept (blank name/avatar).
  > * GroupDetail.tsx optimistic addable removal on failed addMember.
  > 
  > Verification: pnpm typecheck + lint + test (API tests DB-backed). Generated by Claude Code multi-agent review.

- `13:24` `[LINEAR]` **gonglx8**: DRP-49 "Refactor sweep (2026-06-05): 47 verified code-quality clusters" [Done] (Improvement)
  > Tracking issue for the `/simplify-refactor` pass run on 2026-06-05. A massively-parallel, behavior-preserving code-quality review (236 agents) over the whole repo excluding `archive/`, build output, generated migrations, and tests-as-production.
  > 
  > **No features, no bug fixes, no behavior changes.** Every cluster below survived an adversarial skeptic agent prompted to refute it.
  > 
  > ## Pipeline
  > 
  > * 236 agents -> **275 raw findings** -> **112 clusters** -> **47 survived adversarial verification**.
  > * Full write-up (problem, change steps, before/after sketch, files, effort/risk/impact per cluster): `docs/refactor/2026-06-05-refactor-report.md` in the repo.
  > 
  > ## Headline wins
  > 
  > * **Performance (highest raw impact):** two N+1 query-storm clusters - `groups.mine/get` and `events.mine/get` - load per-row data inside loops; bulk loaders are the biggest individual win.
  > * **The 982-line** `events.ts` **god-file:** the "open the blind moment" transition is encoded twice (auto-lock vs manual-lock); the `events.mine`/`events.get` caller-derivation preamble is copy-pasted; several helpers are misnamed.
  > * **Mobile dedup + theme drift:** date/time parsing duplicated between `lib/format.ts` and `ui/DateTimeField`; deadline math duplicated across `CreateWizard`/`Dashboard`/`EventDetail`; hardcoded hex/spacing literals that should use `theme.ts` tokens; user-facing copy inlined in screens instead of `lib/copy.ts`.
  > * **Type-chain consistency:** per-screen `Awaited<ReturnType<...>>` instead of a shared `RouterOutputs`; bare-string wizard step keys instead of a typed union; duplicated text-length bounds across Zod schemas.
  > 
  > Effort skews small: 40 S, 6 M, 1 L. All low-or-medium risk.
  > 
  > ## Top of the roadmap
  > 
  > | \# | Epic | Effort | Risk | Impact |
  > | -- | -- | -- | -- | -- |
  > | 1 | Export shared RouterOutputs/RouterInputs from lib/trpc.ts instead of per-screen Awaited<ReturnType<...>> | S | low | med |
  > | 2 | Single-source the test-DB connection coordinates + terminate/drop sweep | S | low | med |
  > | 3 | Lift deadlineMs into status.ts; route Dashboard + EventDetail through it | S | low | med |
  > | 4 | Make wizard step keys a shared typed union | S | low | med |
  > | 5 | Fix N+1 query storms in groups.mine/get (+ shared user-card projection) | M | low | high |
  > | 6 | Rename misleading getter/action/field helpers in events.ts | S | low | med |
  > | 7 | Rename EventDetail's lookalike state pairs (editNote/editNotes, editing/editable) | S | low | med |
  > | 8 | Single-source text-length bounds across input schemas | S | low | med |
  > | 9 | Replace DateTimeField's private parse helpers with the shared lib/format contract | M | low | med |
  > | 10 | Replace events.mine/get per-plan N+1 loads with a shared bulk loader | L | med | high |
  > 
  > The remaining 37 clusters (naming, dead-code, theme-token adoption, copy centralization, smaller dedup) are detailed in the report. To execute: pull a cluster from the report and run `/simplify-refactor apply` or implement directly - each is independently shippable in one commit. Run `pnpm lint && pnpm typecheck && pnpm test` before each PR.

- `15:19` `[LINEAR]` **gonglx8**: DRP-50 "M4: group onboarding - invite links + join codes + first-run" [Done] — assignee gonglx8
  > M4 goal: make BeThere a complete product by letting a real person actually get into a group. Today the only "add member" path is the seeded-demo-user picker (`groups.addableUsers`), so a friend who installs the app can't be added - there is no invite, join code, or link anywhere.
  > 
  > Design (brainstormed): WhatsApp/Partiful-style **shareable link + short human-typeable join code** per group, on top of the existing Google sign-in. **Web-first** (Expo web on Vercel is the live surface): a web invite is just a URL you open + sign in; the join **code** is the universal primitive that also works on native and survives a flaky link. The same path-based `/join/<code>` link becomes a native universal link later with zero backend change.
  > 
  > Scope (all of it):
  > 
  > * **Join bridge**: `groups.invite_code` column + generator; `groups.joinByCode`, `groups.inviteByGroup` procedures; React Navigation linking (`join/:code`); `pendingInvite` continuity across the web OAuth redirect; `JoinGroup` screen; invite BottomSheet in GroupDetail (code + share + copy); "Join a group" entry in GroupsList.
  > * **First-run polish**: real display names in rosters (capture Clerk name on upsert + `users.updateProfile` + editable name in Account); zero-group empty-state guidance.
  > * **Create-during-onboard**: after creating a group, land on its shareable invite.
  > * Remove the seeded picker from the primary UI (keep behind dev flag for tests).
  > 
  > Plan file: /Users/gong/.claude/plans/we-finished-milestone-3-tranquil-quasar.md
  > Approach: design workflow -> backend -> mobile -> adversarial review workflow -> verify. Commit in modular chunks on `dev`.

  > * #12: the `Conditional`-type reuse half was applied; the `ActivityText.trim()` half was deliberately skipped (it would shift `addCandidate`'s max-length validation boundary - a real behavior change - since the handler already trims).

- `11:03` `[DISCORD #prezzy]` **the_jlh**: DRP - Final Presentation
  > University friend groups have trouble organising meetups, typically due to poor communication. From our interviews and our survey of 46 people, we found out that it is often the case people usually wait to see who else responds before they decide, leading to friction in the group chat. The Problem

- `12:26` `[LINEAR]` **gonglx8**: DRP-55 "Split events.ts into router + membership/lifecycle/bundle modules" [Done]
  > PROPOSAL from the 2026-06-09 pass-2 simplify-refactor (cluster #14). Left unapplied because it is the one **medium-risk** cluster and the highest-churn item, and it overlaps the three events.ts clusters that DID land this pass ([DRP-54](https://linear.app/drp-02/issue/DRP-54/refactor-pass-2-structural-simplify-refactor-2026-06-09) #1/#2/#7: normalizeCreateCandidates, resolveCreateDeadlines, get()-projection helpers, clearMyReactions + applyCas).
  > 
  > > **Re-scope before applying:** the line numbers below are from BEFORE pass-2. events.ts has since shrunk (create() decomposed, deadline + projection helpers extracted to `logic/create-candidates.ts` / `logic/event-schedule.ts`), so `resolveMomentEnd` already lives in `logic/event-schedule.ts` and `startMinute`/`activityKey` in `logic/create-candidates.ts`. Re-derive anchors by content against current `events.ts` (\~970 lines now).
  > 
  > ---
  > 
  > ## Problem
  > 
  > `apps/api/src/routers/events.ts` is 1101 lines. A \~430-line module-level helper layer sits ahead of the tRPC router. Sibling routers stay thin (`groups.ts` \~200, `users.ts` \~21) and the project already factors API logic into `logic/plan-activity.ts`, `logic/past-meetups.ts`, `db/groups.ts`, `auth/resolve.ts`. The helper layer is three cohesive, independently-extractable concerns, and `groups.ts` imports `requireMember` out of the router file (a router<->router smell).
  > 
  > This is a pure cut-paste-and-import extraction. No logic, call-site, or wire-shape changes.
  > 
  > ## Plan
  > 
  > 1. `db/event-membership.ts` (new): move `requireMember` + `loadEvent`. Repoint `groups.ts`'s import. Must depend on nothing in the other new modules to avoid a cycle.
  > 2. `logic/event-lifecycle.ts` (new): move `fizzle`, `settlePhase`, `openMoment`, `settleCollecting`, `settleLifecycle`, `settleAndRequirePhase` plus the shared primitives/readers/constants they share with the router and bundle (`responsesFor`/`reactionsFor`/`candidatesFor`, `isTimeCand`, `activityCandidateInputs`, `goingFromRow`, `isReadyToLock`, `byStartsAtThenNullsLast`, `toMomentResponse`/`toReaction`, `insertCandidates`/`ensureReaction`/`clearOptOut`/`clearMyResponse`/`clearMyReactions`, constants, `EventRow`/`DbOrTx`). Note: `resolveMomentEnd` already moved to `logic/event-schedule.ts` in pass-2 - import it.
  > 3. `db/event-bundle.ts` (new): move `loadEventBundle`, `derivePlanView`, `computeBaseStatus`, `computeMyStatus`, `groupBy`, `reactionCountsFor`/`projectCandidates`, `MyStatus`.
  > 4. `routers/events.ts`: delete moved defs, import them; file drops to imports + `eventsRouter`.
  > 5. Verify: `pnpm --filter @bethere/api typecheck`, `pnpm db:up && pnpm test`, `pnpm lint`.
  > 
  > ## Must stay UNCHANGED (behavior preservation)
  > 
  > * Every function body verbatim; every procedure; all Zod input schemas; all returned wire shapes.
  > * Settle order `settleCollecting` then `settlePhase`; `openMoment`'s guarded compare-and-set; always-anonymous creator self-check.
  > * `requireMember` stays exported; `.js` extensions on all relative imports (ESM).
  > * No cycle: `event-membership.ts` imports neither lifecycle nor bundle.
  > 
  > ## Verified facts (pre-pass-2)
  > 
  > * Only direct cross-module import of an events.ts internal: `groups.ts` -> `requireMember`.
  > * All 16 `events-*.test.ts` files (DB-backed) run through `appRouter.createCaller` via `test/harness.ts` - no test imports an events.ts internal, so extraction breaks no tests.
  > 
  > Effort L, risk med (bounded by the DB-backed events suite), impact med. Report: `docs/refactor/2026-06-09-refactor-report-pass2.md`.

- `15:33` `[LINEAR]` **gonglx8**: DRP-56 "Meetup-centric share link + web-first conversion funnel" [Done]
  > Maximize conversion of the rest of the group chat: turn a meetup link dropped in a chat into a signed-up, responding group member, web-first. From the Luca M4 interview (adoption friction / web seamlessness).
  > 
  > Funnel: `/m/<eventId>` -> public meetup preview BEFORE sign-in -> one-tap "Join {Group} & respond" -> Google sign-in -> joins the event's group + lands on the meetup -> respond.
  > 
  > This iteration the joiner becomes a NORMAL group member. Ephemeral guests (respond without joining) are deferred to the next iteration.
  > 
  > Slices:
  > 
  > 1. Backend: events.previewByToken (public), joinByToken, shareLink + meetupUrlFor + tests
  > 2. Client pure helpers: meetup.ts, webview.ts, copy
  > 3. JoinMeetup funnel screen
  > 4. Linking + Gate logged-out pivot + pending-meetup routing (riskiest)
  > 5. Share affordance (EventDetail + post-create)
  > 6. OG unfurl card: static brand tags (must-ship) + per-meetup Vercel function
  > 7. In-app-browser escape hatch
  > 8. Inline create-group in the wizard group step
  > 9. Soft "get the app" nudge after responding
  > 
  > Conversion levers in scope: per-meetup OG unfurl card + in-app-browser/OAuth handling. Deferred: ephemeral guests, notifications, dynamic OG image, Google One-Tap, group-optional meetups.
  > 
  > Plan: /Users/gong/.claude/plans/cheeky-tumbling-cocke.md. Target: Final Demo 16-17 Jun.
- `16:02` `[LINEAR]` **gonglx8**: DRP-57 "PostHog integration (focused): server Family-D telemetry + web-first client funnels" [Backlog]
  > Focused PostHog integration (NOT "full PostHog"). Out of scope: session replay, feature flags, A/B testing, surveys, client autocapture.
  > 
  > **Goal:** produce the Family-D product-outcome telemetry defined in `docs/m4/quantitative-evaluation.md` section 3.4 (currently `[TEAM TO FILL - log on staging / live]`). Headline metric: plan clear rate.
  > 
  > **Decisions:**
  > 
  > * Identity: pseudonymous `distinct_id` = internal userId (opaque, never PII, in-app anonymity untouched).
  > * Host: PostHog Cloud EU.
  > * Client: web-first cross-platform (`posthog-js` web, `posthog-react-native` native), autocapture + replay OFF.
  > * Server (`posthog-node`) is the metric backbone; env-gated no-op when unconfigured (tests stay hermetic).
  > 
  > **Server choke points (apps/api/src/routers/events.ts):** `create` -> plan_created; `openMoment` (won race) -> moment_opened; `settlePhase`/`fizzle` -> plan_cleared/plan_fizzled; `respond` -> rsvp_submitted; `toggleReaction` -> candidate_voted; `addCandidate` -> candidate_added.
  > 
  > Design spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-06-09-posthog-integration-design.md`

- `12:07` `[LINEAR]` **gonglx8**: DRP-58 "Deadlines step copy is ambiguous - adopt the voting/RSVP vocabulary" [Done] — assignee gonglx8
  > User feedback on the create-wizard Deadlines step: the language ("Decides by", "Decides Sat 27 Jun", "Reply by", "Blind until then, then it reveals who's in") is extremely ambiguous - it never says WHAT decides, WHO replies, or what happens at each deadline.
  > 
  > Fix: speak the vocabulary the live screens already use (`DEADLINE_VOTING` = "Voting closes", `DEADLINE_RSVP` = "RSVP closes" from `lib/copy.ts`), with each card stating the time plus the consequence in plain English. Also update the confirm-step summary lines and the "Decides soon" notification to the same vocabulary.
- `12:08` `[LINEAR]` **gonglx8**: DRP-59 "Adding a time on the voting screen can throw the full-screen server error" [Done] — assignee gonglx8
  > User feedback: adding a time on the voting (collecting) screen sometimes throws a server error instead of being handled gracefully.
  > 
  > Root cause: `events.addCandidate` rejects times at/before `decidesBy` or past the plan's horizon. The date/time pickers bound the DATE but the combined date+time can still land out of range (e.g. min date Sat 27 Jun but a time earlier than the 13:03 deadline). On rejection, `runAction` (`useBusyAction`) does `setError(true)`, which swaps the WHOLE screen for the full-screen `DetailError` until the next 5s poll clears it.
  > 
  > Fix:
  > 
  > 1. Validate the composed date+time client-side in `AddTime` (mirror the server's two rules), disabling Add with a note quoting the actual boundary.
  > 2. Mutation failures on EventDetail surface as an inline `FormError` + reload-to-reconcile, never the full-screen error (that is for load failures only). Known server rejects map to friendly lines.
- `12:10` `[LINEAR]` **gonglx8**: DRP-60 "Voting board: "+ add a time" / "+ add an activity" triggers look like plain text, inconsistent with the UI" [Done] — assignee gonglx8
  > User feedback on the collecting (voting) screen: the "+ add a time" and "+ add an activity" affordances are bare pink text links, which are unclear and inconsistent with the rest of the neobrutalist UI (everything else tappable is a bordered row/card/button).
  > 
  > Fix: restyled the collapsed `AddComposer` trigger (`apps/mobile/src/ui/AddComposer.tsx`) as a dashed-border "empty slot" row in the same geometry as the candidate `Row`s above it (same radius `ui.rInput`, border width 2, padding 11/13), with the bold-13 ink label. It reads as the next empty row in the table - tap to fill. Open state (input + Add/Cancel) unchanged; copy unchanged. Both lists share the one component, so one change fixes both.
  > 
  > **Done** in commit `6610854` on `dev` - lint, typecheck, mobile tests (205), and quality gate all green.

- `12:37` `[LINEAR]` **gonglx8**: DRP-61 "Deadlines step: full first-time comprehension - timeline layout + half-hour default deadlines" [Done] — assignee gonglx8
  > Follow-up to [DRP-58](https://linear.app/drp-02/issue/DRP-58/deadlines-step-copy-is-ambiguous-adopt-the-votingrsvp-vocabulary) after the user pushed on whether the relabelled screen was truly unmistakable for a first-time creator. Two residual problems:
  > 
  > 1. **Default deadlines leaked the creation instant's minutes** ("Voting closes Sat 27 Jun, 13:03" / "05:03") - reads as machine noise and makes the screen feel arbitrary.
  > 2. **Two side-by-side cards still made the user assemble the lifecycle themselves** (vote closes -> winner locked -> blind RSVP -> reveal).
  > 
  > Fix:
  > 
  > * Floor both deadline defaults to a half-hour boundary (shared helper + mobile mirror identically; exact instant kept when flooring would cross the after-now/after-open bound). Side benefit: absorbs render-vs-create clock drift between the wizard preview and the server's stored default.
  > * Rebuild the step as a 3-stage drawn timeline (numbered ink dots + rail): "You send it" (anonymity + what the group votes on, computed per plan shape), "Voting closes" (consequence: most-voted time/activity wins, meetup locked), "RSVP closes" (blind until close, then everyone sees who's in). Concrete plans get a 2-stage variant; a no-times plan keeps stage 3 as a plain explainer instead of it vanishing.

- `14:44` `[LINEAR]` **gonglx8**: DRP-62 "Ad-hoc & cross-group meetups (freely-composed roster)" [Done]
  > Decouple a meetup's audience from a single group's membership, per the M4 Luca interview.
  > 
  > **Model (owner-approved):** a meetup's roster is the freely-composed **live union** of (origin group members) ∪ (additional attached groups' members) ∪ (ad-hoc individuals who joined by link). Groups stay live (a new group joiner flows into the meetup automatically); individuals are ephemeral, plan-scoped, and never appear in "My Groups". Individuals are added **by link only** (no people directory). A cleared meetup can be **crystallized into a permanent group**.
  > 
  > **Data model (additive, no backfill migration):** keep `events.groupId` as the origin group; add `event_groups(eventId, groupId)` and `event_participants(eventId, userId)`. When both are empty the behavior is byte-for-byte today's.
  > 
  > **Core change:** a single `rosterUserIds(eventId, originGroupId)` helper replaces `memberIdsOf(groupId)` / `requireMember`, used by the 5 read paths (`events.get` members, the membership gate, `events.mine`, quorum/`resolveIn`, the conditional picker + reveal). The vote engine (`candidateReactions`/`responses`/`eventOptOuts`, already `(eventId,userId)`-keyed) does not move. `joinByToken` flips from inserting a `group_members` row to an `event_participants` row.
  > 
  > **New flows:** `events.addGroup`, `groups.createFromEvent`, and the compose affordances on the plan (reusing the [DRP-56](https://linear.app/drp-02/issue/DRP-56/meetup-centric-share-link-web-first-conversion-funnel) share sheet for individuals).
  > 
  > **Horizon:** demoable for the Friday M4 progress meeting. Deferred: fully groupless meetups (nullable groupId + migration), "people you've met" picker, per-group name-hiding, remove-group/un-invite.
  > 
  > Design spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-06-10-adhoc-cross-group-meetups-design.md`

- `16:18` `[LINEAR]` **gonglx8**: DRP-63 "Iteration 5: +1 trust controls - joins lock, brought-by attribution, Who's-in roster (Nathan M4 interview)" [Done] — assignee gonglx8
  > Iteration 5, driven by the Nathan M4 interview (`docs/drp-context/interviews/m4 interviews/nathan interview.md`).
  > 
  > Interview signals:
  > 
  > 1. "Adding +1s has to be a controlled thing" - he discovered a +1 can re-share the meetup link, so chains are unbounded.
  > 2. Awareness with a timing edge: "between when I vote and when the votes are tallied, someone else gets invited" - the failure mode is showing up and a stranger is there.
  > 3. (Deferred) "this person has been to an event with you" recognition signal.
  > 
  > Scope (3 slices, met-before deferred):
  > 
  > * **Joins lock**: `events.joinsOpen` (door, default open) + `events.lockJoins` (set at suggestion; locked = frozen for everyone, like lockTimes/lockActivity; unlocked = any group member can toggle the door later). Enforced in `joinByToken`; a closed link still previews but won't admit new people. Existing roster members always no-op-rejoin fine.
  > * **Brought-by attribution**: `shareLink` mints `?via=<userId>`; `joinByToken` validates via against the live roster and stores `event_participants.invitedBy` (+ `joinedAt`). Soft attribution (bearer link), deliberately not a security boundary - the lock is.
  > * **Who's-in sheet**: full roster on EventDetail - origin group, attached groups, and +1s with "via X's link" attribution and a "new since you last looked" badge (client-side seen marker).
  > 
  > Design decisions logged in the spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-06-11-plus-one-trust-controls-design.md`. Approach 1 (query-param via) chosen over a per-sharer token table - identical forwarding semantics, far smaller diff.
